I need to keep absolute control over my food, so I would never allow myself to eat a large amount.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight, or are you currently underweight because of how much you restrict eating?\n\nPatient: My campus doctor and RA keep saying I've gotten way too thin and my clothes are hanging off me, but I honestly don't feel sick enough for it to be a big deal. When I look in the mirror, I still see soft areas that make me feel like I need to keep my strict calorie limit. I'm still getting A's and walking to class, so it feels like everyone is blowing this out of proportion.\n\nDoctor: When you think about your weight and shape, do you feel intense fear of gaining weight or becoming fat, even if other people say you are too thin?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, absolutely—the thought of eating normally makes me panic because I'm terrified I'll blow up overnight and everyone will see me as lazy and out of control. Even though everyone keeps telling me I'm already "so thin," when I look in the mirror I still see soft areas and feel like I need to lose a little more before I can finally relax.

Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, I’m Dr. Chat. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I'm really only here because my campus doctor and advisor insisted on it. I just feel like if I don’t keep really tight control over what I eat, I’ll gain weight and everything I’ve worked so hard for here will completely fall apart.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had episodes where you eat a large amount of food in a short time and then try to get rid of it by vomiting, using laxatives, fasting, or excessive exercise?\n\nPatient: No, I've never done anything like that. It's actually the exact opposite for me because I strictly limit my intake to a very tight calorie budget, like just black coffee, half a granola bar, and a tiny salad. I need to keep absolute control over my food, so I would never allow myself to eat a large amount.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight, or are you currently underweight because of how much you restrict eating?\n\nPatient: My campus doctor and RA keep saying I've gotten way too thin and my clothes are hanging off me, but I honestly don't feel sick enough for it to be a big deal. When I look in the mirror, I still see soft areas that make me feel like I need to keep my strict calorie limit. I'm still getting A's and walking to class, so it feels like everyone is blowing this out of proportion.\n\nDoctor: When you think about your weight and shape, do you feel intense fear of gaining weight or becoming fat, even if other people say you are too thin?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely—the thought of eating normally makes me panic because I'm terrified I'll blow up overnight and everyone will see me as lazy and out of control. Even though everyone keeps telling me I'm already \"so thin,\" when I look in the mirror I still see soft areas and feel like I need to lose a little more before I can finally relax.\n\nDoctor: Have your menstrual periods become irregular or stopped since you started restricting your food intake?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't really kept track of my periods lately because my schedule has been so hectic and I've been under a lot of academic stress.\n\nDoctor: Have your periods become less frequent or stopped altogether since you started restricting food and losing weight?\n\nPatient: I really haven't paid attention to it, so I honestly can't say if they've stopped or just gotten irregular. I've been so completely focused on my honors classes and art studio work that I haven't kept track of that at all.\n\nDoctor: Since your weight loss and food restriction started, have your menstrual periods become less frequent or stopped altogether?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know because I just haven't been paying attention to that at all. With all the stress of my classes and keeping up with my schedule, my period is the last thing on my mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight and are you currently underweight because of restricting food, or is your weight still within a normal range?\n\nPatient: The campus doctor did tell me I'm underweight, and my clothes are hanging off me from losing weight. But I don't feel sick enough for it to be a big deal since I still have energy for my classes, and when I look in the mirror, I still see soft areas that make me feel like I need to lose more.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight and are you currently below a healthy weight because of restricting food, or is your weight still in the normal range?"

plans.\n- Chief Complaint: I feel like if I don’t keep really tight control over what I eat, I’ll gain weight and everything I’ve worked for will fall apart.\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S043 (Restrictive_Intake): Most days I’ll have black coffee in the morning, maybe half a granola bar between classes if I feel faint, and then just pick at a tiny salad in my dorm at saying I’ve gotten “so thin,” but when I look in the mirror I still see places that look soft to me, so I keep thinking I should lose just a little more before I can relax, even though my clothes are hanging off me and I’m always cold in class.\n- S045 (Fear_of_Weight_Gain): If I even imagine eating like a normal college student—pizza at a study group or late-night ice cream with my roommates—I get this panicky feeling that I’ll blow up overnight and all the work I’ve put into being disciplined and successful here will disappear and people will see me as lazy and out of control.\n- S046 therapist, but if it goes up even half a pound I feel disgusting and like I don’t deserve to be in any of my roles on campus.\n- S084 (Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition): When the campus doctor talked about my heart and bone health and mentioned maybe taking a medical leave, it honestly felt blown way out of proportion to me, because I walk to all my classes, I’m still getting A’s, and apart from being tired and lightheaded sometimes, I don’t feel sick enough for it to be this serious.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: I’ve started skipping club meetings and Even though everyone keeps telling me I'm already \"so thin,\" when I look in the mirror I still see soft areas and feel like I need to lose a little more before I can finally relax.\n\nDoctor: Have your menstrual periods become irregular or stopped since you started restricting your food intake?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't really kept track of my periods lately because my schedule has been so hectic and I've been under a lot of academic stress.\n\nDoctor: Have your periods become less frequent or stopped altogether since you started restricting food and losing weight?\n\nPatient: I really haven't paid attention to it, so I honestly can't say if they've stopped or just gotten irregular. I've been so completely focused on my honors classes and art studio work that I haven't kept track of that at all.\n\nDoctor: Since your weight loss and food restriction started, have your menstrual periods become less frequent or stopped altogether?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know because I just haven't been paying attention to that at all. With all the stress of my classes and keeping up with my schedule, my period is the last thing on my mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight and are you currently underweight because of restricting food, or is your weight still within a normal range?\n\nPatient: The campus doctor did tell me I'm underweight, and my clothes are hanging off me from losing weight. But I don't feel sick enough or in any danger, especially since I can still walk to class and get A's. When I look in the mirror, I still see soft areas and feel like I need to lose a little more before I can relax.\n\nDoctor: Have your periods stopped for at least three months since you started losing weight or restricting food? I've been so stressed and busy with my classes that it's not something I've paid attention to.\n\nDoctor: Have your periods stopped for at least three months, or become much less frequent, since you started restricting food and losing weight?\n\nPatient: Like I said, I really haven't been keeping track of my periods at all. I've been so consumed with my schoolwork and keeping control of my weight that it just hasn't been on my radar."

I need to keep absolute control over my food, so I would never allow myself to eat a large amount.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight, or are you currently underweight because of how much you restrict eating?\n\nPatient: My campus doctor and RA keep saying I've gotten way too thin and my clothes are hanging off me, but I honestly don't feel sick enough for it to be a big deal. When I look in the mirror, I still see soft areas that make me feel like I need to keep my strict calorie limit. I'm still getting A's and walking to class, so it feels like everyone is blowing this out of proportion.\n\nDoctor: When you think about your weight and shape, do you feel intense fear of gaining weight or becoming fat, even if other people say you are too thin?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely—the thought of eating normally makes me panic because I'm terrified I'll blow up overnight and everyone will see me as lazy and out of control. Even though everyone keeps telling me I'm already \"so thin,\" when I look in the mirror I still see soft areas and feel like I need to lose a little more before I can finally relax.\n\nDoctor: Have your menstrual periods become irregular or stopped since you started restricting your food intake?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't really kept track of my periods lately because my schedule has been so hectic and I've been under a lot of academic stress.\n\nDoctor: Have your periods become less frequent or stopped altogether since you started restricting food and losing weight?\n\nPatient: I really haven't paid attention to it, so I honestly can't say if they've stopped or just gotten irregular. I've been so completely focused on my honors classes and art studio work that I haven't kept track of that at all.\n\nDoctor: Since your weight loss and food restriction started, have your menstrual periods become less frequent or stopped altogether?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know because I just haven't been paying attention to that at all. With all the stress of my classes and keeping up with my schedule, my period is the last thing on my mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight and are you currently underweight because of restricting food, or is your weight still within a normal range?\n\nPatient: The campus doctor did tell me I'm underweight, and my clothes are hanging off me from losing weight. But I don't feel sick enough for it to be a big deal since I still have energy for my classes, and when I look in the mirror, I still see soft areas that make me feel like I need to lose more.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight and are you currently below a healthy weight because of restricting food, or is your weight still in the normal range?\n\nPatient: The campus doctor told me I've lost a lot of weight and am underweight, and my clothes are definitely hanging off me. But I don't feel sick enough or in any danger, especially since I can still walk to class and get A's. When I look in the mirror, I still see soft areas and feel like I need to lose a little more before I can relax.\n\nDoctor: Have your periods stopped for at least three months since you started losing weight or restricting food? If you don't get periods, please tell me that too.\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure, because I haven't really thought about it or tracked my periods lately. I've just been so overwhelmed with school and my schedule that I really can't say.\n\nDoctor: Have your periods stopped for at least three months, or have they become much less frequent since you started restricting food and losing weight?\n\nPatient: I honestly can't tell you because I just haven't been tracking my periods at all. I've been so stressed and busy with my classes that it's not something I've paid attention to.\n\nDoctor: Have your periods stopped for at least three months, or become much less frequent, since you started restricting food and losing weight?\n\nPatient: Like I said, I really haven't been keeping track of my periods at all. I've been so consumed with my schoolwork and keeping control of my weight that it just hasn't been on my radar.\n\nDoctor: Have your periods stopped for at least three months, or become much less frequent, since you started restricting food and losing weight?\n\nPatient: I really am not sure because I haven't kept track of my periods lately. I've been so completely overwhelmed and focused on my classes and my daily routine that I just haven't paid any attention to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a lot of weight and are you currently below a healthy weight, or has your weight stayed in the normal range despite the restriction?\n\nPatient: My campus doctor says I am underweight and my clothes are hanging off me, and I do find myself always feeling cold in class. However, when I look in the mirror I still see soft areas and feel like I need to lose a bit more, so everyone's concern feels really exaggerated since I'm still getting good grades."

I need to keep absolute control over my food, so I would never allow myself to eat a large amount.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight, or are you currently underweight because of how much you restrict eating?\n\nPatient: My campus doctor and RA keep saying I've gotten way too thin and my clothes are hanging off me, but I honestly don't feel sick enough for it to be a big deal. When I look in the mirror, I still see soft areas that make me feel like I need to keep my strict calorie limit. I'm still getting A's and walking to class, so it feels like everyone is blowing this out of proportion.\n\nDoctor: When you think about your weight and shape, do you feel intense fear of gaining weight or becoming fat, even if other people say you are too thin?\n\nPatient: Yes, absolutely—the thought of eating normally makes me panic because I'm terrified I'll blow up overnight and everyone will see me as lazy and out of control. Even though everyone keeps telling me I'm already \"so thin,\" when I look in the mirror I still see soft areas and feel like I need to lose a little more before I can finally relax.\n\nDoctor: Have your menstrual periods become irregular or stopped since you started restricting your food intake?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure, to be honest. I haven't really kept track of my periods lately because my schedule has been so hectic and I've been under a lot of academic stress.\n\nDoctor: Have your periods become less frequent or stopped altogether since you started restricting food and losing weight?\n\nPatient: I really haven't paid attention to it, so I honestly can't say if they've stopped or just gotten irregular. I've been so completely focused on my honors classes and art studio work that I haven't kept track of that at all.\n\nDoctor: Since your weight loss and food restriction started, have your menstrual periods become less frequent or stopped altogether?\n\nPatient: I honestly don't know because I just haven't been paying attention to that at all. With all the stress of my classes and keeping up with my schedule, my period is the last thing on my mind.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight and are you currently underweight because of restricting food, or is your weight still within a normal range?\n\nPatient: The campus doctor did tell me I'm underweight, and my clothes are hanging off me from losing weight. But I don't feel sick enough for it to be a big deal since I still have energy for my classes, and when I look in the mirror, I still see soft areas that make me feel like I need to lose more.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight and are you currently below a healthy weight because of restricting food, or is your weight still in the normal range?\n\nPatient: The campus doctor told me I've lost a lot of weight and am underweight, and my clothes are definitely hanging off me. But I don't feel sick enough or in any danger, especially since I can still walk to class and get A's. When I look in the mirror, I still see soft areas and feel like I need to lose a little more before I can relax.\n\nDoctor: Have your periods stopped for at least three months since you started losing weight or restricting food? If you don't get periods, please tell me that too.\n\nPatient: I'm honestly not sure, because I haven't really thought about it or tracked my periods lately. I've just been so overwhelmed with school and my schedule that I really can't say.\n\nDoctor: Have your periods stopped for at least three months, or have they become much less frequent since you started restricting food and losing weight?\n\nPatient: I honestly can't tell you because I just haven't been tracking my periods at all. I've been so stressed and busy with my classes that it's not something I've paid attention to.\n\nDoctor: Have your periods stopped for at least three months, or become much less frequent, since you started restricting food and losing weight?\n\nPatient: Like I said, I really haven't been keeping track of my periods at all. I've been so consumed with my schoolwork and keeping control of my weight that it just hasn't been on my radar.\n\nDoctor: Have your periods stopped for at least three months, or become much less frequent, since you started restricting food and losing weight?\n\nPatient: I really am not sure because I haven't kept track of my periods lately. I've been so completely overwhelmed and focused on my classes and my daily routine that I just haven't paid any attention to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a lot of weight and are you currently below a healthy weight, or has your weight stayed in the normal range despite the restriction?\n\nPatient: My campus doctor says I am underweight and my clothes are hanging off me, and I do find myself always feeling cold in class. However, when I look in the mirror I still see soft areas and feel like I need to lose a bit more, so everyone's concern feels really exaggerated since I'm still getting good grades.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel that your self-worth is strongly tied to your weight or body shape, so that gaining weight would feel like a major failure?\n\nPatient: Yes, my self-worth is completely tied to the scale and how my body looks. If the scale goes down, I feel worthy of the honors program and my future career, but if it goes up even half a pound, I feel disgusting, lazy, and like a total failure who doesn't deserve to be on campus. Gaining weight makes me panic because it feels like I'm losing control of everything I've worked so hard to achieve."
  }

Subtypes: Derailment or Loose Associations, Tangentiality, Incoherence or Word Salad, Neologisms, Clang Associations\n[S022] Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior: A state where behavior and motor control are severely disturbed, appearing as movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation.  Subtypes: Childlike Silliness, Stupor, Catalepsy, Waxy Flexibility, Mutism, Negativism, Posturing, Mannerism, Stereotypy, Agitation, Grimacing, Echolalia, Echopraxia\n[S023] Negative_Symptoms: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.  Subtypes: Diminished Emotional Expression, Avolition, Alogia, Anhedonia, Asociality\n[S024] Depressed_Mood: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.  Subtypes: Depressed Mood, Tearfulness, Sadness, Emptiness, Emotional Numbness\n[S025] Loss_of_Interest: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.  Subtypes: Loss of Interest, Social Withdrawal, Loss of Motivation, Anhedonia, Indifference to Achievement\n[S026] Weight_Appetite_Change: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.  Subtypes: Significant Weight Loss, Weight Gain, Decrease in Appetite, Increase in Appetite\n[S027] Sleep_Disturbance: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.  Subtypes: Initial Insomnia, Middle Insomnia, Early Morning Awakening, Hypersomnia, Nightmare Disorder, Non-Restorative Sleep\n[S028] Psychomotor_Changes: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.  Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.  Subtypes: Chronic Fatigue, Physical Fatigue, Mental Fatigue, Loss of Energy\n[S030] Worthlessness_Guilt: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).  Subtypes: Feelings of Worthlessness, Ruminative Guilt, Excessive or Inappropriate Guilt, Delusional Guilt\n[S031] Diminished_Thinking: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.  Subtypes: Reduced Concentration, Indecisiveness, Subjective Memory Complaints, Clouded Thinking\n[S032] Death_Suicide: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt. Subtypes: Elevated Mood, Irritable Mood, Mood Lability\n[S034] Increased_Activity: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.  Subtypes: Increased Energy, Hyperactivity, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S035] Inflated_Self_Esteem: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions). Subtypes: Reduced Sleep Duration, Loss of Sleep Need, Indifference to Insomnia\n[S037] Pressured_Speech: More talkative than usual or pressure to keep talking.  Subtypes: Talkativeness, Rapid Speech, Pressured Speech\n[S038] Racing_Thoughts: Flight of ideas or subjective experience that thoughts are racing.  Subtypes: Racing Thoughts, Flooding of Thoughts, Inability to Control Thoughts\n[S039] Manic_Distractibility: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.  Subtypes: Responsiveness to Extraneous Stimuli, Frequent Task Switching, Inability to Sustain Attention\n[S040] Increased_Goal_Activity: Increase in goal-directed activity (at work, school, socially, or sexually) or purposeless psychomotor agitation.  Subtypes: Occupational Hyperactivity, Social Hyperactivity, Hypersexuality, Psychomotor Agitation\n[S041] Painful_Consequence_Behavior: Excessive involvement in activities that have a high potential for painful consequences. Subtypes: Eating Very Small Amounts, Skipping Meals, Exclusion of Specific Food Groups, Strict Calorie Restriction\n[S044] Low_Weight: A body weight that is significantly below the minimum level of health for age, sex, developmental trajectory, and physical health. Subtypes: Fasting, Excessive Exercise\n[S048] Compensatory_Behavior_Purging: Recurrent inappropriate compensatory behaviors involving the expulsion of food or fluids to prevent weight gain.  Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities. Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge. Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. (Note: In children, may be expressed by crying, tantrums, freezing, or clinging).  Subtypes: Animal Type, Natural Environment Type, Blood-Injection-Injury Type, Situational Type, Other Type\n[S063] Immediate_Fear/Anxiety_Response: The phobic object or situation almost always provokes immediate fear or anxiety.
